  **Backend:** `joshx_db.py` — `clients` (name/company/contact/email/phone/instagram/website/industry/client_type/lead_source/status/last_contact_date/next_follow_up_date/relationship_strength/notes), `leads` (client_name/project_description/service/estimated_value/budget/lead_source/probability/stage/follow_up_date/notes), `projects` (client_name/project_name/project_type/brief/start_date/due_date/shoot_date/budget/priority/status/deliverables/notes) — one free-text status/stage field per table, no DB-level enums, same "kept deliberately small" philosophy as `alpha_mode_db.py`'s crew/equipment tables. `joshx_tools.py`: seven Frank tools (`add_joshx_client`/`update_joshx_client_status`/`log_joshx_client_contact`/`add_joshx_lead`/`update_joshx_lead_stage`/`add_joshx_project`/`update_joshx_project_status`), same shape as `personal_tools.py` — deliberately no `consult_joshx_agent` delegated specialist, same "stay in Frank's own voice, don't risk drifting into unsolicited advice" call already made for Personal. `build_joshx_block()` folds current clients/leads/projects into Frank's system prompt directly. New `GET /joshx/dashboard` computes real counts only — Active Projects, Open Leads, Upcoming Shoots (shoot_date within 14 days) — and deliberately omits Revenue/Outstanding/Available Days, since Phase 1 has no invoices or availability data model and this app never shows a stat without real data behind it (the standing Mission Status fake-progress-bar precedent).

  **Desktop:** New `JoshxView.swift` — header (JOSHX / Joshua Peters / Freelance Filmmaker · Video Editor · Photographer), a real stats row, an honest note that Revenue/Outstanding/Availability arrive in a later phase, then PROJECTS/LEADS/CLIENTS sections with real empty states pointing at Frank as the only entry point right now ("tell Frank about a Joshx booking to get started"). New shared `PCorpKit` types (`JoshxClientRecord`/`JoshxLead`/`JoshxProject`/`JoshxDashboard`) and `JoshxClient`, same one-shot GET pattern as `PersonalClient`/`TradingDivisionClient`. Nav placement needed real care: the spec's own example shows Joshx directly under Alpha Mode Media in the sidebar, but `PlaceholderData.navItems`' array position drives `ContentView`'s Cmd+1–9 shortcuts — inserting Joshx there would have silently bumped every later shortcut (Automations losing Cmd+9, etc.). Fixed by appending Joshx to the *end* of the flat array (position 11, past Cmd+9's reach, same as Triggers/Calendar/Settings already were) while placing it visually right after "Alpha Mode Media" in `Sidebar.swift`'s `DIVISIONS` navGroup — the display-layer/array-position split this app's grouping mechanism was already built for. Visual identity: `Sidebar.swift`'s icon color special-cases "Joshx" to `theme.accent` (the Face-Lift's own new intelligence-signal color) instead of `IconColors`' fixed system-color palette, mirroring how Alpha Mode Media already gets a special-cased brand color there — the one deliberate identity marker, not a new design system. No division switcher, no shared dashboard, no toggle, no "current division" selector — exactly per the spec's explicit "DO NOT BUILD" list. iOS untouched, per the standing "desktop first, mobile once everything's complete" instruction.

  Verified: a direct DB round-trip test (add a client/lead/project, confirm `dashboard_snapshot()`/`summarize()` both correct, real R-formatted currency) before ever touching the app, then the test rows deleted so Joshua's real `joshx.db` starts genuinely empty — not seeded with test data. Backend restarted via the supervised `launchd` service, `GET /joshx/dashboard` curl-tested clean. `swift build` (PCorpKit + desktop) clean, the packaged `.app` rebuilt and relaunched — confirmed live: Joshx appears as a fully separate DIVISIONS destination with its own accent-tinted icon, the dashboard renders real (currently zero) stats and correct empty-state copy, and Alpha Mode Media's own section is untouched and still works exactly as before.

## 2026-08-24

- **Finance — the third and final previously-declined section, built for real this time.** Joshua asked to watch five real accounts (Liberty Stash, EasyEquities, Luno, Ashburton Stable Income Fund, Nasdaq/Markets), get daily updates, and eventually have Frank flag lucrative opportunities "with my permission." Investigated the actual technical landscape before writing any code, since this determines what "watch my investments" can honestly mean: **only Luno has a real official API** — a scoped, revocable read-only key generated in Luno's own settings, not an account password. EasyEquities has no official API at all; only unofficial community tools exist, and every one of them works by logging in with the user's real EasyEquities username/password — declined outright, not built, since storing or auto-submitting a real brokerage password is exactly the credential handling this system is built to avoid, official-API-or-not. Liberty Stash and Ashburton have no API of any kind (Ashburton's own investor platform was also found to be retiring 1 July 2026 in favor of FNB's Investor Platform — flagged to Joshua directly since it may affect an account he hasn't checked recently). Nasdaq/Markets holdings would need a broker-specific API, not just market price data. Confirmed the resulting scope directly with Joshua: Luno automatic, the other four via manual updates (same honest "Josh tells Frank, Frank logs it" pattern already proven for Joshx/Personal) — and opportunity-scanning deferred entirely as its own future round, given it needs real market-data sourcing and careful scoping around what Frank can flag without crossing into actual investment advice (this system's own standing rule against giving personalized financial advice applies to Frank too).

  **Backend:** new `backend/app/finance_db.py` (own `finance.db`, same "genuinely separate domain" pattern as `joshx.db`/`personal.db`) — an `accounts` table pre-seeded with Josh's actual five accounts (`is_automatic=1` only for Luno) and a `balance_snapshots` table (account, asset, balance, timestamp) allowing **multiple assets per account** — Luno in particular can hold ZAR cash and crypto (XBT, ETH, …) simultaneously, and this app never sums mismatched currencies into one fabricated "total portfolio" number, same discipline as Joshx's Revenue/Outstanding omission. `dashboard_snapshot()` computes a real up/down/flat trend per asset by comparing each account's two most recent snapshots — genuinely derived, not invented. New `backend/app/luno_client.py`: plain `httpx` call to Luno's real documented `GET /api/1/balance` endpoint with HTTP Basic Auth (key ID/secret from `.env`, same credential-storage pattern as every other API key this app already holds), fails soft (empty list) on any error including missing credentials. New `backend/app/finance.py`: `maybe_snapshot_luno()`, gated to run at most once per day — rather than starting a second background loop for one more daily job, this rides the *same* 15-minute scheduler tick the Triggers Layer already introduced (`main.py`'s `_trigger_scheduler_loop`), consistent with this codebase's own restraint principle against adding infrastructure before it's needed. New `backend/app/finance_tools.py`: one Frank tool, `log_finance_balance`, for the four manual accounts — deliberately no `consult_finance_agent` delegated persona, doubly justified here since a persona commentating on Josh's portfolio would risk exactly the kind of unsolicited investment advice this system avoids giving; Frank just records and displays what it's told. New `GET /finance/dashboard`. Real bug caught and fixed before it ever reached Swift: `is_automatic` came back from SQLite as a raw Python `int` (0/1), which FastAPI serializes as a JSON number, not a JSON boolean — Swift's `Bool: Decodable` would have failed outright on a real request, not just looked wrong. Fixed by explicitly casting to `bool()` in `dashboard_snapshot()`, same fix already applied once before for `triggers_db.py`'s `enabled` field.

  **Desktop:** new shared `PCorpKit` types (`FinanceHolding`/`FinanceAccount`/`FinanceDashboard`) and `FinanceClient`, same one-shot GET pattern as `JoshxClient`. New `FinanceView.swift` — one card per account, a live trend arrow (green up / red down / grey flat) plus asset code and balance per holding, an "AUTOMATIC" badge on Luno, and honest empty-state copy pointing at Frank as the entry point for the four manual accounts. No nav-structure changes needed at all: "Finance" has occupied a real slot in `PlaceholderData.navItems`, `Sidebar.swift`'s `DIVISIONS` group, and `IconColors` (orange) since the section was first scaffolded — it was always a placeholder waiting for this exact build, not a new destination like Joshx/Triggers needed to be. Just one new `case "Finance": FinanceView()` in `ContentView.swift`'s switch.

  Verified: a direct DB round-trip test (two logged balances, confirmed the trend correctly computed "down") before touching the app, then cleared so Joshua's real `finance.db` starts genuinely empty. Backend restarted via the supervised `launchd` service, `GET /finance/dashboard` curl-tested clean, the `is_automatic` boolean bug caught and re-verified via curl before writing any Swift. `swift build` (PCorpKit + desktop) clean, the packaged `.app` rebuilt and relaunched — confirmed live: all five accounts render with correct honest empty states, Luno's AUTOMATIC badge shows correctly, and every other section is untouched. Luno's live API integration itself is not yet exercised end-to-end — `LUNO_API_KEY_ID`/`LUNO_API_KEY_SECRET` aren't in `.env` yet, so the daily snapshot job currently no-ops exactly as designed (fails soft, no crash) until Joshua generates a read-only key and adds it.

- **Luno made real and automatic — surfaced two genuine bugs along the way, one of them a pre-existing architectural issue that predates Finance entirely.** Joshua generated a read-only API key in Luno's own settings (permissions restricted to balance-read only, no IP restriction or expiry — both would risk silently breaking the integration later for a security benefit the read-only scope already provides) and added it to `.env`.

  **Bug one — Luno's real balance shape doesn't match one-asset-one-number.** First live test against Joshua's actual account showed Luno splits each asset across multiple sub-accounts (Transactional/Staking/Bundle/Earn/Prediction) — e.g. ADA came back as three separate entries (0, 0, 11.004303), SOL as two (0.07044232, 0.00208966). `finance.py`'s `maybe_snapshot_luno()` was logging each sub-account entry as its own snapshot row under the same asset code, which would have silently corrupted `finance_db.py`'s trend math (it only compares the two most recent rows per asset) — whichever sub-account happened to be inserted last would become "the" balance, with real held value from the others just vanishing from the trend. Fixed by summing all sub-account balances per asset before logging, so each asset gets exactly one honest snapshot per day representing the true total across every Luno sub-account.

  **Bug two — the dual-listener architecture silently double-runs the whole backend's startup, not just this feature.** After the fix, every asset still showed up logged *twice* with identical timestamps. Root cause, once traced: `main.py`'s `run()` starts two independent `uvicorn.Server` instances against the same `app` object whenever `TAILSCALE_IP` is set (`_run_dual`, for local + Tailscale listeners — a real, intentional feature from 2026-07-31, not a mistake). Each server's ASGI lifecycle independently enters `lifespan()` on startup — so without a guard, the scheduler task (`_trigger_scheduler_loop`, the same one driving the Triggers Layer's daily digest) was being created twice, two competing copies racing in one process, plus a second `httpx.AsyncClient` for ElevenLabs silently leaked and never closed. This has been true since the Triggers Layer's scheduler was first built — Finance's Luno snapshot is just what made it visible, being the first job whose double-execution left an obvious, checkable artifact (duplicate rows with identical timestamps). Fixed with an `app.state` guard (the one object genuinely shared between both lifespan entries, since both servers wrap the same `app`): the scheduler task and the ElevenLabs client are now only created if they don't already exist, and only torn down once. Confirmed the fix didn't affect the Triggers Layer itself — `GET /triggers/status` re-checked clean after the change.

  Verified end to end: stale duplicate/pre-fix rows cleared from `finance.db` twice (once after each bug fix), backend restarted clean each time, direct Python calls against `luno_client.fetch_balances()` confirmed real authenticated data (17 real assets — XBT, ETH, ADA, DOT, SOL, XRP, several tokenized US stocks like AAPLx/SPYx/QQQx, and more), then `GET /finance/dashboard` confirmed exactly one row per asset with correct summed values (SOL's two sub-account balances summing to `0.07253198`, matching by hand). Not yet re-confirmed visually in the packaged `.app` — the app window didn't come to the foreground in this session for a screenshot, but the data layer both feeds and the UI reads from is fully verified via the API directly.

- **Luno's overall rand value — requested by Joshua after he noticed the Finance tab only showed per-asset lines, no total.** A naive sum across mismatched assets (0.000071 XBT + 11.004303 ADA + ...) would be meaningless, but a real ZAR value computed from live market prices is legitimate math, not the fabrication this app otherwise avoids. New `luno_client.py`'s `fetch_zar_prices()`: Luno's own public `/tickers` endpoint (no auth needed, unlike the balance call), covering every asset Luno quotes directly against ZAR. New `finance.py`'s `compute_luno_zar_value()`, wired into `GET /finance/dashboard` so the Luno account carries a real `luno_value` (`estimated_zar_value`, `priced_assets`, `unpriced_assets`) alongside its per-asset holdings, and a new `LunoValueSummary` in `FinanceView.swift` shows the total prominently at the top of the card. First version covered only 9 of Josh's 17 real assets — Josh asked why immediately, a fair catch. Investigated rather than guessed: Luno's tokenized-stock products (AAPLx, SPYx, QQQx, TQQQx, VTIx, GLDx) have zero pricing anywhere on Luno itself (confirmed against all 145 of its real live pairs), and BNB/PAXG are only quoted against USDT there, never ZAR directly. Closed both gaps with real data, not guesses: BNB/PAXG get a legitimate cross-rate (their USDT price × Luno's own live USDTZAR rate — one extra real multiplication using Luno's own numbers); the xStock holdings turned out to be a shared tokenization standard also tracked independently by CoinGecko, confirmed live via CoinGecko's own public search API for each of Josh's exact holdings before writing anything, then priced through new `coingecko_client.py` (free, public, no API key, direct ZAR conversion built into CoinGecko's own API). Two real bugs caught and fixed before either shipped: (1) the cross-rate conversion initially did nothing at all — `USDTZAR` was being matched by the general `pair.endswith("ZAR")` branch before ever reaching the specific `== "USDTZAR"` check, so the branch that actually captured the conversion rate was unreachable code; reordered so the exact-match check runs first. (2) None, on the CoinGecko side — confirmed each of the 6 coin IDs (`apple-xstock`, `sp500-xstock`, `nasdaq-xstock`, `tqqq-xstock`, `gold-xstock`, `vanguard-xstock`) against CoinGecko's real search results before hardcoding the mapping, rather than guessing at an ID pattern. Verified end to end: direct Python calls confirmed `fetch_zar_prices()` returning real BNB/PAXG prices only after the reordering fix, `GET /finance/dashboard` re-curled showing all 17 assets priced and `unpriced_assets` empty (total **R1,951.66**, up from the first version's incomplete R684.99/R685.58 covering only 9 assets), then the packaged `.app` relaunched and confirmed live — the Luno card's total updates and its "excludes..." note correctly disappears now that nothing is actually excluded.

- **Calendar goes read/write — Frank can now create, reschedule, and cancel real events, not just show them.** Joshua asked to "integrate my calendar so Frank can update it for me when I need to." The read side already existed (`desktop/Sources/PCorpOS/SystemCalendar.swift`, AppleScript automation against the real macOS Calendar app — confirmed 2026-07-26 that EventKit silently denies access without a proper app bundle/Info.plist, so AppleScript was already the proven path, not a new decision to revisit). The write side is new, and runs server-side in the Python backend (new `backend/app/system_calendar.py`) via the same `osascript` mechanism, not through the Swift app — the backend already runs locally on Josh's own Mac as a supervised `launchd` service, so it can shell out directly with no cross-process bridge needed; confirmed this live before writing anything real. New `backend/app/calendar_tools.py`: four Frank tools (`create_calendar_event`, `update_calendar_event`, `delete_calendar_event`, `list_calendar_events`) — deliberately no attendees/invites support at all, since adding one would mean Frank silently sending notifications to other people, which is "send a message on Josh's behalf" territory (this app's standing permission boundary for every domain), not a local reversible action like the rest of what these tools do. `update_calendar_event` only touches the fields actually given, matched against the soonest upcoming event whose title contains the identifier text (never an ancient past event by title collision — bounded to a 90-day forward window).

  Three real bugs found and fixed live before this shipped, none guessed at: (1) `tell default calendar` is not valid AppleScript for Calendar.app at all — confirmed directly (`get name of default calendar` throws "A class name can't go after this identifier") — fixed to `item 1 of calendars` when Josh doesn't name a specific one. (2) Rescheduling both the start and end date of an event in one call could fail outright with "The start date must be before the end date" — Calendar.app validates start < end on *every individual property write*, not just the final state, so setting a new start date past the event's still-old end date (or vice versa) gets rejected even though the caller's final values are perfectly valid together; fixed by routing the end date through a temporary far-future placeholder first, so every intermediate write stays valid regardless of which direction both dates move. (3) A stray empty-string typo in the two AppleScript output separator constants was caught before it ever ran — turned out the file already had the real control characters (`\x02`/`\x01`, matching the Swift read path's own `\u{2}`/`\u{1}`) despite how the source looked at a glance, confirmed by reading the raw bytes rather than trusting the visual diff. Verified end to end: a full create → rename+reschedule → delete round trip tested directly against the real Calendar app (confirmed both the rename and the time change landed correctly), then the exact same sequence re-tested through `calendar_tools.py`'s dispatch layer with the same tool-call shapes Frank actually uses, then a real live conversation with Frank through the packaged `.app` — asked in plain language to add an event tomorrow at 3pm, Frank checked the calendar for conflicts first (unprompted, per the tool description's own guidance), created the real event, and replied "Done — P Corp OS Live Test, tomorrow (Aug 25) 3:00–3:30pm. Clear of your 9-11am Factory Shoot." Confirmed live in Calendar.app itself, then removed as test data. One real, known side effect worth noting: writing an event brings Calendar.app to the foreground and steals window focus from PCorpOS (a consequence of the `open -a Calendar` step that ensures the app is actually running before AppleScript can talk to it) — not fixed this round, flagged rather than silently left for Josh to discover.

- **Finance's three manual accounts logged for real, then HF Markets made automatic too.** Joshua gave real current balances for Liberty Stash (R6,700), EasyEquities (R2,877), and Nasdaq/Markets (R505.84, from a live screenshot of the actual MT5 terminal), logged directly via `finance_db.log_balance()` — Finance's data is now genuinely populated across every account except Ashburton. That screenshot revealed something that changed the automation story entirely: HF Markets isn't accessed through some third-party platform — Josh already runs MetaTrader 5 locally on this same Mac via `net.metaquotes.wine.metatrader5` (the official MetaQuotes Wine wrapper), hosting his real `ICT_NDX_EA` trading robot live. Earlier research had found HF Markets' own official API (FIX) requires a $200,000 minimum deposit, and the only realistic alternative was trusting a third-party bridge like MetaApi.cloud with investor credentials — a real, separate trust decision. An already-running local terminal makes that unnecessary.

  Instead: new `PCorpBalanceExport.mq5`, a small read-only MQL5 Expert Advisor placed directly in the terminal's own Experts folder (found live: `.../MetaTrader 5/MQL5/Experts/`, right alongside `ICT_NDX_EA.mq5`). It only calls `AccountInfoDouble`/`AccountInfoString`/`AccountInfoInteger` (queries) and `FileWrite`, on a 60-second timer — never places, modifies, or closes a trade, and runs alongside `ICT_NDX_EA` without touching it. Writes a plain JSON file (balance, equity, currency, login, timestamp) into the terminal's own sandboxed `MQL5/Files` folder. New `backend/app/hf_markets_client.py` reads that file directly — no credentials, no network call, no third party at all, the same "local file, no API needed" simplicity as Trading Division's own read of `research.sqlite`. New `finance.py`'s `maybe_snapshot_hf_markets()` rides the same shared 15-minute scheduler tick as Luno's own snapshot, gated to once a day, fails soft if the file doesn't exist yet (the EA hasn't been compiled/attached in MT5 yet — a real manual step only Josh can do, walked through directly: MetaEditor → compile → drag onto any chart → check "Allow Algo Trading"). `finance_db.py`'s `Nasdaq / Markets` account flipped to `is_automatic=1`, with a migration (`UPDATE accounts SET is_automatic = 1 WHERE name = 'Nasdaq / Markets'`) so the existing row picks it up without a fresh seed. Verified: the manual balance logging round-tripped through the real dashboard endpoint correctly (all three accounts showing real figures, `is_automatic` correctly flipped for Nasdaq/Markets via a re-curl after restart), and `read_balance()` confirmed correctly returning `None` right now, before Josh has compiled/attached the EA — the fail-soft path proven before the real one is. Activation itself (compiling in MetaEditor, attaching to a chart) is a real step only Josh can take in his own MT5 terminal, not yet done as of this writing.

  **Activated same day, with one real near-miss caught in time.** Walking Josh through attaching the EA, MT5 itself threw a real warning: dragging `PCorpBalanceExport` onto the same chart already running `ICT_NDX_EA` would *replace* it — only one EA runs per chart in MT5, so confirming that dialog would have silently stopped his actual live trading robot. Caught before clicking through it; instead attached to a separate new chart, leaving `ICT_NDX_EA` completely undisturbed on its own. Once actually attached: `read_balance()` immediately started returning real live data (`{"login": 54887701, "balance": 505.84, "equity": 395.72, "currency": "ZAR", ...}`), `maybe_snapshot_hf_markets()` correctly logged a real snapshot and marked the day's schedule done, and `GET /finance/dashboard` confirmed it live end to end — Nasdaq/Markets now shows `is_automatic: true` with a real, current balance. HF Markets joins Luno as the second fully-automatic Finance account; Liberty Stash, EasyEquities, and Ashburton remain manual (Ashburton still has no balance logged at all).

- **All five Finance accounts populated with real data; live equity/floating P&L added for HF Markets.** Joshua gave real current balances for Liberty Stash (R6,700), EasyEquities (R2,877), and Ashburton Stable Income Fund (R2,471), logged directly the same way — Finance now shows real figures across every single account, none still empty.

  Separately, asked for HF Markets' live balance to reflect open trades, with updates when in profit or loss. Scoped directly first, since "update me" could mean a few different things architecturally (push via the Triggers email digest vs. just visible on demand): confirmed **live in the Finance tab only**, no push notification for now. New `finance.py`'s `get_hf_markets_live_status()` reads `hf_markets_client.py`'s file **fresh on every dashboard request** (never stored/snapshotted, same "don't cache a number that goes stale the moment the market moves" reasoning already applied to Luno's own rand-value total) — returns `balance` (the settled figure the daily snapshot tracks), `equity` (reflecting open positions), and `floating_pnl` (their difference: positive = currently in profit, negative = currently in loss). Wired into `GET /finance/dashboard` as a new `hf_markets_live` field. New shared `HFMarketsLiveStatus` type and a `HFMarketsLiveSummary` view in `FinanceView.swift` — shows live equity prominently with a green/red floating-P&L figure right next to it, plus the settled balance and last-updated time underneath. Verified against real live data through the actual backend endpoint, watched update in real time as the market moved (`floating_pnl` genuinely changed from `-119.44` to `-121.70` between two consecutive checks, confirming this is truly live, not cached) — a macOS Desktop-folder permission dialog blocked a fresh in-app screenshot this round, so the UI itself wasn't re-confirmed visually, though the same `HoldingRow`/`LunoValueSummary`-pattern rendering code was already proven correct in every earlier Finance round.

- **Real bug caught live by Joshua: Luno's total silently dropped 6 assets after a transient CoinGecko failure.** A screenshot showed the Luno card back down to `R784.36` with `AAPLX, GLDX, QQQX, SPYX, TQQQX, VTIX` excluded — the exact gap that was supposedly closed the same day. Root cause: `coingecko_client.py`'s `fetch_xstock_zar_prices()` was fetching fresh on *every single* `GET /finance/dashboard` call with no cache and no fallback — a retest moments later succeeded cleanly (200, real prices), confirming this was a one-off transient hiccup (a free-tier rate-limit brush or a slow response), not a real ongoing outage, but the fail-soft design meant that one bad request instantly made 6 real assets vanish from the total for that entire page load. Fixed with a 5-minute in-memory cache that, critically, **falls back to the last known-good prices on a failed refresh instead of returning empty** — a personal portfolio doesn't need sub-5-minute price freshness, and a stale-but-real price is far more honest than a silently incomplete total. Verified by simulating a real failure directly (pointed the client at a nonexistent endpoint after a successful fetch) and confirming it correctly returned the cached prices rather than dropping them; then confirmed live against the real backend that all 17 assets are priced again (`unpriced_assets: []`, total back to `R1,959.84`).

## 2026-08-25

- **"Always look for lucrative investment opportunities" — the last deferred piece of Joshua's original Finance ask, built as a new Triggers rule: Market Movers.** Scoped directly with him first, given real safety stakes: this system is built to never give personalized investment advice, so the actual feature is a "biggest movers" screener — the same category as a stock screener's "today's gainers" list — never a recommendation. Confirmed two real decisions before writing code: scope covers assets **outside** Josh's current holdings too (not just what he already owns — accepted specifically because staying purely factual makes this safe regardless of scope, an asset he doesn't own is exactly as "not advice" as one he does), and delivery **reuses the Triggers Layer** entirely rather than a new mechanism — gets the daily email digest, decay/dedup cadence, and the existing Triggers UI toggle for free.

  **Universe, deliberately bounded, not an unbounded market scan:** all 26 assets Luno quotes directly against ZAR, plus the 25 xStock products CoinGecko tracks (confirmed live against CoinGecko's real search API before hardcoding the id map, same discipline as the 6-asset map built for Finance's Luno total) — both sources this app already has real, working access to, nothing invented for this feature. New `coingecko_client.py` function `fetch_all_xstock_zar_prices()` sits alongside the existing holdings-only one, sharing the same cache-with-fallback pattern from the bug fixed earlier the same week.

  **New `backend/app/market_movers.py`:** a real price-history mechanism was needed (nothing tracked market prices over time before this — `finance_db.py`'s `balance_snapshots` tracks account balances, not market prices, and correctly wasn't reused for a concept that isn't tied to any one account). New `market_price_snapshots` table in `triggers_db.py` (lives there, not `finance_db.py`, since a market price isn't a Finance-account concept), snapshotted once a day via the same shared scheduler tick as Luno/HF Markets' own balance snapshots. `check_market_movers()` compares each asset's latest price against the closest snapshot ≥1 day old, flags anything moving ≥10% (`NOTABLE_MOVE_THRESHOLD_PCT`, hardcoded like every other threshold-less rule, not a new configurable column). Registered as a new `market_mover` rule_type in the existing `trigger_rules`/`RULE_CHECKERS` machinery — no Swift changes needed at all, since `TriggersView.swift` already renders every rule generically (confirmed via grep: zero hardcoded rule-type strings in the Swift UI).

  **The safety framing is deliberate throughout, not incidental:** labeled `"MARKET MOVERS"` in the UI, never `"OPPORTUNITIES"`; item copy is strictly factual (`"XBT up 25.0%"` / `"R1,271,247.00, was R1,016,997.60 1d ago"`) with no language implying suitability, timing, or a call to action anywhere in the code or copy. `item_key` includes the date (`market_mover:{asset}:{date}`) rather than just the asset — a market move is a today-specific fact, not a persistent condition like an overdue invoice, so there's nothing to decay/resurface on day 3/7 the way the Triggers Layer's cadence does for other rules; each day's move is simply a new, distinct item, and the existing "first sighting = always due" dedup logic already handles that shape correctly with no special-casing.

  Verified end to end: backend restart confirmed `market_mover` seeded correctly via `GET /triggers/rules`; the snapshot job and checker tested directly (correctly found 0 movers with only one day of price history, no false positives); a real detection test — inserting a genuine historical price 2 days back at 80% of XBT's current price — correctly produced `{"title": "XBT up 25.0%", "detail": "R1,271,247.00, was R1,016,997.60 1d ago"}`, then cleaned up as test data and reconfirmed 0 movers; `GET /triggers/status` re-curled showing the new `"MARKET MOVERS"` section correctly wired in alongside the four existing rules.

- **iOS Face-Lift parity, sixth pass: Triggers, Joshx, and Finance ported — closes the gap between platforms on all three major features built since the original parity work.** Joshua asked directly for mobile to catch up on everything it was missing. Audited first rather than assuming: Finance already had a `NavItem`/`Sidebar` slot from the original parity pass but no view (fell through to the honest `SectionPlaceholderView`); Triggers and Joshx had neither. Calendar needed nothing — it's already a real, working EventKit-backed browser on iOS, and calendar *writes* live entirely in the backend (`calendar_tools.py`), which Frank already uses identically regardless of which client is connected.

  All three new views (`FinanceView.swift`, `TriggersView.swift`, `JoshxView.swift`) are near-verbatim ports, not reimplementations — confirmed none of the three desktop source files have any AppKit dependency before copying, same discipline already established for `AlphaModeDashboardView`'s own port. Every type they depend on (`FinanceClient`/`TriggersClient`/`JoshxClient` and their models) was already shared in `PCorpKit`, built cross-platform from day one — this pass only needed the three SwiftUI view files themselves plus nav wiring. `NavItem.swift` gained `Joshx` (after Alpha Mode Media) and `Triggers` (after Automations), matching desktop's exact placement; `Sidebar.swift`'s `navGroups` updated to match, plus the same `theme.accent` icon special-case desktop's own Sidebar uses for Joshx. `RootView.swift`'s switch gained three new cases.

  Also ported the proactive greeting (Face-Lift item 08) that iOS never got in the original parity pass — its War Room greeting was still the static time-of-day line only. iOS's version reuses the same `insightsClient` already feeding this view's own "FRANK'S INSIGHTS" card, rather than desktop's separate `greetingInsightsClient` — a deliberate simplification, not an oversight: desktop needs a second client because the greeting and the right-rail Insights card are two different views there; on iOS they're the same view, so one fetch already covers both.

  Verified: `xcodebuild` clean for the iOS Simulator destination (zero errors across every new/changed file), the app built, installed, and launched successfully on a real booted simulator (iPhone 17) — reached its "Connect to Frank" pairing screen correctly. Full visual confirmation past that screen was blocked by a real tooling limit hit live: the native iOS Simulator control tool needs a one-time `sudo xcode-select` fix only Joshua can run, and the scripted-click fallback into the Simulator app's own window was also blocked (macOS Accessibility permission, error -25200) — flagged directly rather than silently skipped. In its place, the one thing that *would* crash the app on a real mismatch — `Sidebar.swift`'s `#if DEBUG` assertion that `navGroups` and `NavItem.items` contain the exact same title set — was checked by direct comparison of both lists (13 items each, identical), not assumed.

  **A real, pre-existing bug found live during verification: the hamburger button couldn't be tapped at all.** After finally getting a live simulator session running (working through the tooling blockers above), neither a scripted click nor Joshua's own real tap could open the sidebar. Read the code rather than assuming it was another environment quirk: `RootView.swift`'s edge-swipe-to-open hot zone (added 2026-08-13 to fix a real "closing worked, opening didn't" bug) was a `ZStack`-level sibling spanning the *entire screen height* — which put its leading 32pt strip directly on top of `topBar`'s hamburger button, silently swallowing every tap on the button itself, not just the swipe gesture it was built for. This predates today's session entirely; it just had never been tapped directly before (only ever opened via edge-swipe, which happened to work fine since the swipe gesture and the button's tap gesture were both hitting the same zone). Fixed by moving the hot zone to a `.overlay(alignment: .leading)` on just the content `Group` (below `topBar`/`SystemStatusHeader`) instead of the whole `ZStack` — preserves the exact gesture-arbitration fix the 2026-08-13 comment describes (still sits above `WarRoomView`'s `ScrollView` in z-order, so opening-by-swipe still works), while no longer overlapping the header row above it. Verified live: Joshua confirmed his own tap opened the sidebar immediately after the fix, then confirmed `Joshx` renders its real data correctly on iOS (client "Herbish," project "Herbish - Factory Machinery Video," 2 real leads, correct stats) — the same backend, same data, same `theme.accent` styling as desktop.

## 2026-08-27

- **"Frank does not respond when I use the mobile app," reported live.** Investigated every layer before touching anything: backend healthy locally and over Tailscale (both 200 OK), the `launchd` service running, Tailscale itself confirmed connected on both ends (`tailscale status --json` showing the Mac and Joshua's iPhone both `Online: True`), the real device confirmed connected via `xcrun devicectl`, and a fresh rebuild's CodeSign step confirmed the provisioning profile hadn't hit its usual 7-day expiry. Deployed a fresh build to the real device and asked Joshua to test live while streaming its console — the console capture itself came back empty (a separate, unexplained tooling gap, not investigated further since a more direct signal was available), so checked `backend/data/pcorp.db` directly instead: it showed a genuine, freshly-timestamped round trip ("Frank do you respond now?" → "Yeah, I'm here, Josh. What's up?"), meaning the backend had processed and answered a real message correctly. Joshua confirmed live that the reply actually reached his screen this time. Root cause was never pinned to one exact line — the working theory is a stale install missing fixes already made earlier in this same project's history — but a from-scratch rebuild + reinstall + relaunch resolved it, verified against real backend data rather than assumed.

- **Real bug found live in the same session: couldn't scroll through chat history while the keyboard was open.** Joshua asked directly why he couldn't "swipe the keyboard up and down" — clarified this meant scrolling the chat thread while typing, not dismissing the keyboard itself. Read `WarRoomView.swift` rather than guessing: `dashboardHeader` (greeting, `WarRoomCommandMap`, Mission Status, Insights) sat as a fixed-height sibling *outside* `ChatThreadView`'s own `ScrollView`. With the keyboard closed there's enough screen height for both; with it open, SwiftUI's `VStack` gives the fixed header its full natural height regardless, squeezing the flexible `ScrollView` below it down — potentially to near-zero — leaving no real draggable area over the actual messages. Fixed by folding `dashboardHeader` (and the divider beneath it) into `ChatThreadView`'s own `ScrollView` as the first item ahead of the message list, so header and messages are one continuous scrollable region: `ChatThreadView` now takes a `@ViewBuilder header` closure instead of assuming the header lives outside it. Verified live: Joshua confirmed both this fix and the "Frank doesn't respond" fix together on the real device.

- **iOS Face-Lift parity, seventh and final pass — closes every remaining gap between desktop and mobile.** Joshua asked directly to bring mobile fully up to date. Audited both platforms' file lists side by side rather than assuming: only two nav sections still fell through to the honest `SectionPlaceholderView` (`Trading Division`, `Personal`), and one entire feature — voice OUTPUT — existed on desktop with no iOS equivalent at all.

  `TradingDivisionView.swift` and `PersonalView.swift` are near-verbatim ports, same discipline as every prior pass: confirmed neither desktop source file has an AppKit dependency, confirmed every type they use (`TradingDivisionClient`/`PersonalClient` and their models) was already shared cross-platform in `PCorpKit`, so this needed only the two view files plus two new `RootView.swift` switch cases. With these wired in, **all 13 of iOS's nav sections are now real** — the `default: SectionPlaceholderView` branch is unreachable today, kept only as a safety net rather than an active fallback.

  **Voice output was the bigger piece — iOS could listen but never talk back.** Desktop's `VoiceOutput.swift` (ElevenLabs TTS via the backend's `/speak` proxy) had no iOS port; iOS's own `FrankOrb.swift` even documented the gap explicitly ("One real state NOT ported: `.speaking`... no real audio signal for a speaking state to react to on iOS today"). Ported `VoiceOutput.swift` near-verbatim (`AVFoundation`/`AVAudioPlayer` are fully cross-platform, needed no rework there) with one real, necessary fix rather than a blind copy: desktop's `speakURL` hardcodes `127.0.0.1`, correct there since the backend runs on the same Mac, but wrong on a phone — swapped for `BackendHost.host`, the same Tailscale-IP mechanism `BackendClient.swift` already uses for chat, since a hardcoded loopback address would silently never reach the Mac from a real device. Wired into `WarRoomView.swift` with the exact same mechanics as desktop: a `pendingVoiceReply` flag set only on a voice-triggered send, consumed once `backend.isStreaming` flips back to `false` (so only the complete reply is spoken, never a mid-stream fragment), and `voiceOutput.stop()` called when a new push-to-talk recording starts (cutting Frank off mid-sentence, same as interrupting a person). `FrankOrb.swift` gained the `.speaking(audioLevel:)` state it was missing, identical to desktop's (particles push outward scaled by real playback amplitude, replacing the "no honest signal, don't build it" placeholder), plus a new `voiceOutput.isSpeaking` / `voiceOutput.errorMessage` pair of view states in `WarRoomView.swift` mirroring the existing `voiceInput.isListening` / `.errorMessage` ones.

  One fix needed beyond a straight copy: `VoiceOutput.swift` uses `@Published` inside an `NSObject`-backed `ObservableObject`, which needs `import Combine` explicitly on iOS (desktop's build picks it up transitively through `AppKit`, iOS's doesn't) — caught immediately as a build error, not shipped broken. `xcodebuild` clean for the real device, installed and relaunched successfully.

  **A second real bug found live during verification: "Frank heard me but didn't reply."** Joshua tested push-to-talk on the real device — the backend's own log confirmed the full round trip actually worked (`POST /speak` returned `200 OK`, and `pcorp.db` showed a genuine, correctly-generated reply), so the reply text and audio both existed; they just never appeared on screen. Root cause, once traced: `VoiceInput.swift` sets the shared `AVAudioSession` to a mic-only `.record` category for speech-recognition accuracy, and never resets it — a session's *category* persists across `setActive(false)`, only activation itself toggles. A `.record`-only category can't route audio output at all, so when `VoiceOutput` tried to play Frank's reply, `AVAudioPlayer.play()` silently failed to actually start (its return value was being discarded, uninspected) — meaning `audioPlayerDidFinishPlaying` never fired either, since real playback never began. That left `isSpeaking` stuck `true` forever, which pinned `WarRoomView`'s UI on the full-screen "Speaking…" orb indefinitely, permanently hiding the chat thread underneath — including the reply text that had, in fact, already arrived. Desktop never hits this since macOS has no equivalent per-category output gate. Fixed by having `VoiceOutput.speak()` explicitly switch the session to a `.playback` category (with `.duckOthers`) and activate it before creating the player, and by checking `play()`'s boolean return value so a genuine failure now surfaces the existing error state instead of hanging silently. Verified live: Joshua confirmed the voice reply now plays correctly end to end.

  Verified live: Joshua confirmed Trading Division and Personal both render correctly on the real device — this pass is fully verified end to end, closing every remaining desktop/iOS gap.

- **Frank's voice, changed to "British guy with eery energy."** `ELEVENLABS_VOICE_ID` (`backend/.env`) is the only thing that controls this — no code change needed, just picking a real voice ID and restarting the backend. First attempt picked a promising-sounding voice ("The Aristocratic Dark Sorcerer") straight from ElevenLabs' own public marketing/category pages (`elevenlabs.io/voice-library/evil`, confirmed against raw page source, not a hallucinated ID) — but it came back **404 Not Found** against the real TTS API. Real, non-obvious platform behavior: a voice ID from ElevenLabs' public Voice Library isn't usable via the API until it's been explicitly added to *this account's* own voice collection — a step that requires being logged into the ElevenLabs dashboard, which Claude can't do on Joshua's behalf (credential/login boundary). The backend's own fail-soft design (`[speak] ElevenLabs failed, falling back to Piper`, confirmed in `/tmp/pcorpos-backend.log`) meant this failed silently into a generic offline fallback voice instead of erroring visibly — correctly avoiding total silence, but also why the first attempt sounded like neither British, deep, nor menacing rather than throwing an obvious error. Corrected by having Joshua browse ElevenLabs' Voice Library himself (he can actually hear previews; text descriptions alone aren't a reliable way to pick a voice), add one to his own account, and hand over the resulting voice ID directly. Verified live: Joshua confirmed the new voice sounds right.

- **Engineering Agent — real codebase access, behind a new approval mechanism.** Joshua asked how to give his agents "more and better capabilities," naming three agents specced in `AGENTS_VISION.md` but never built (Engineering, CFO, Life) and wanting existing agents to get real hands-on-the-codebase capability. Investigated before writing anything: all three "missing" agents turned out to be *deliberately* blocked, not overlooked — `AGENTS_VISION.md`'s own scope note explains Engineering Agent specifically is the general agentic tool-use capability (file access, git) deferred since day one because `SECURITY.md`'s permission/sandboxing model for it didn't exist. Critically, "give Design/Operations/Communications hands-on-the-codebase capability" hits the *exact same wall*. Joshua chose to build the missing infrastructure, with Engineering Agent as its first, proving use case (CFO/Life Agent stay explicitly out of scope — different kind of blocker, policy/privacy rather than infrastructure).

  **Tier classification, applying `SECURITY.md`'s existing three-tier framework to a new tool category rather than inventing one:** Regular/auto-allowed — `read_file`, `list_directory`, `git_log`, `git_diff`, `git_show`, `run_build_check` (two fixed invocations only, never arbitrary shell). Needs explicit confirmation — `propose_file_edit`, which never writes directly: it computes a real diff, sends Joshua an approval card, and blocks until he decides. Prohibited entirely this version, not silently omitted: arbitrary shell, `git commit`/`git push`, editing secrets, deleting files — no tool for any of these exists.

  **New `backend/app/agent_file_safety.py`** — a shared path-boundary helper every file tool resolves through first, refusing anything outside the repo root plus a denylist (`.env` anywhere, `backend/data/`, `.git/` internals, `*.pem`/`*.key`). **New `backend/app/engineering_agent.py`** — architecturally different from every other single-shot specialist agent: it runs its *own* inner tool-use loop (structurally mirroring `main.py`'s own `run_claude_turn`, scoped to a small private toolset Frank never sees) since "read some files, maybe check git log, then propose a specific edit" needs multiple tool round-trips a single completion can't do.

  **The approval flow reuses the existing websocket sentinel convention instead of inventing a new channel** — `BackendClient.swift` already parses `\n[notify]{json}` out-of-band messages inside the normal text stream; added a matching `\n[approval_request]` sentinel, a new `pendingApproval` published property, and a `respondToApproval(approved:)` method that sends `{"approval_response": {...}}` back over the same live socket. `propose_file_edit`'s executor blocks on a direct `await websocket.receive_text()` for that exact reply — safe because nothing else reads the socket while a turn is in-flight. New `ApprovalRequestCard` view on both platforms (reusing `attachedImageChip`'s neutral surface/border styling, deliberately not `SituationRoomBanner`'s red — this is a decision request, not a risk alert), with the input bar disabled while a card is showing.

  **Two real bugs found live during verification, both fixed before shipping:**
  1. Asked the agent to read `main.py` and report commits — it came back with an empty reply, no error. Traced via a direct debug script: `stop_reason: "max_tokens"` after a `thinking`-only content block, with zero visible text — extended thinking was consuming the entire output budget reasoning over a large dumped file, something the other single-shot agents (short, context-light requests) never trigger. Worse, the fix wasn't just "raise max_tokens" — `claude-sonnet-5` rejected the classic `thinking={"type": "enabled", "budget_tokens": N}` shape outright with a real 400 ("not supported for this model, use thinking.type.adaptive and output_config.effort"), a genuine API-shape gap between model generations. Fixed with the model's real adaptive-thinking controls (`thinking={"type": "adaptive"}`, `output_config={"effort": "medium"}`) plus a larger `max_tokens`. Separately, once it started answering, asked for main.py's exact line count and got 578 back against a real 924 — LLMs are unreliable at precisely counting lines from raw dumped text, so `read_file`'s result now leads with a Python-computed `[N lines]` prefix instead of leaving counting to the model; re-verified correct (924) immediately after.
  2. Disconnect-mid-approval test (quit the client while a card was showing): the backend didn't crash, but logged an uncaught `RuntimeError: WebSocket is not connected` instead of a clean shutdown. Root cause: `propose_file_edit`'s blocking `receive_text()` correctly raised `WebSocketDisconnect` on client disconnect, but `main.py`'s existing `except Exception` handler (already there for the unrelated `stopGenerating()` case) caught it generically and `continue`d back to the outer loop's own `receive_text()` — which Starlette doesn't treat as a second `WebSocketDisconnect`, it raises a bare `RuntimeError` instead once a socket's already been marked disconnected once. Fixed with a dedicated `except WebSocketDisconnect: raise` clause ahead of the generic handler, funneling straight into the existing outer `except WebSocketDisconnect: pass` in one clean hop. Re-verified clean: zero errors logged, backend process alive, no stray file written.

  Verified end to end via a direct websocket test script (bypassing UI automation entirely, given known reliability issues with scripted window/screenshot tools this session): real file reads and git history cross-checked against ground truth (`wc -l`, `git log`) and confirmed exactly correct; individual audit rows confirmed for every inner tool call, not just the outer `consult_engineering_agent` call; a proposed edit correctly rejected (file never created) and correctly approved (file created with exact proposed content, `[notify]` confirmation fired); a direct `resolve_repo_path` call refused `backend/.env`, `backend/data/pcorp.db`, `.git/config`, and a `../../` escape attempt while a normal path resolved fine; and Frank correctly refused a live request to read `backend/.env`, no leaked value. New `## Decided (2026-08-27)` entry in `SECURITY.md` and an `Update (2026-08-27)` paragraph in `AGENTS_VISION.md` record the reasoning. Design/Operations/Communications Agents are deliberately not wired into this mechanism yet — an explicit follow-up, not bundled into this change.

  One real, unrelated blocker hit right after shipping: the first live attempt through the desktop app failed with a plain Anthropic API error ("Your credit balance is too low") — the account's API credits had run dry, coincidentally right after this session's own heavy round of Engineering Agent test calls plus the earlier voice/TTS work. Not a bug in anything built here; flagged directly and left for Joshua to top up (billing is his to handle, not something to work around). Verified live once he did: Joshua confirmed a real "review WarRoomView.swift" request through the actual desktop UI worked end to end.

- **A real bug found live right after that: chat text couldn't be copied at all, then only one line at a time.** Plain SwiftUI `Text` isn't selectable by default — neither War Room's chat nor Knowledge's doc viewer had ever opted in. First fix (`.textSelection(.enabled)`) looked complete but the real desktop app kept behaving as if nothing had changed across several redeploys — traced to a genuinely embarrassing gap in this session's own process: the desktop app is packaged by a real script, `desktop/build_app.sh` (copies the fresh executable, embeds the Python runtime, re-signs), which every deploy this session had skipped in favor of plain `swift build` — that only rebuilds the raw SPM executable, never touching the actual `.app` bundle macOS runs. `swift build` reported "Build complete!" every time with zero indication the real bundle was untouched; confirmed only by checking the running binary's file mtime, which was a full day stale. Every desktop-side change from this entire session (the Engineering Agent approval card, the disabled input bar, this text-selection fix) had silently never reached the app Joshua was actually testing. Fixed the deploy process going forward (`build_app.sh`, not `swift build`, then kill + relaunch) and recorded it as its own memory so it doesn't recur.

  With deployment actually fixed, `.textSelection(.enabled)` worked — but only one line at a time. Root cause: `SimpleMarkdownView`'s parser split every line of a reply into its own separate block, and therefore its own separate `Text` view, since SwiftUI only splits it line-by-line rather than treating consecutive plain lines as one soft-wrapped paragraph (real markdown semantics) — and `.textSelection` selects within one `Text` at a time, so a normal multi-sentence reply could only ever be copied a line at a time. First fix merged consecutive plain lines into one paragraph block, which helped but didn't fully solve it — selection still couldn't cross from a paragraph into the next heading or list item, since those remained genuinely separate `Text` views by construction. The real fix was architectural, not incremental: rewrote `SimpleMarkdownView` to build one continuous `AttributedString` for the entire message (heading/paragraph/list-item styling now applied as per-run font/color attributes on one string, with inline bold/italic/links still layered correctly via `AttributedString`'s own `inlinePresentationIntent`) and render it through a single `Text`, so selecting and copying an entire reply now works the same way selecting text in any normal document does. Shared via `PCorpKit`, so both War Room's chat and Knowledge's doc viewer on both platforms got the fix from one change. Verified live: Joshua confirmed both the initial line-level selection and the final whole-message selection work correctly.

- **iOS parity, final pass: New Chat, Chat History, Search, and Mission** were desktop-only in War Room's top bar. Ported `startNewConversation()`/`switchToConversation()`/`fetchConversationList()` (already shared, no `PCorpKit` changes needed) into `RootView.swift`'s top bar and a new `ConversationHistorySheet.swift` — a day-grouped, real content-searchable conversation list (uses native `.searchable` rather than copying desktop's manual search row, the one deliberate iOS-idiom adaptation). Search and Mission ported as real parity, not silently upgraded: both are still no-op shells on desktop itself ("not wired up yet"), so iOS now matches that honestly rather than inventing functionality desktop doesn't have. Mission renders icon-only on iOS rather than desktop's text+icon pill — six buttons plus the hamburger and logo already crowd a phone-width bar. Verified live: Joshua confirmed New Chat and Chat History both work correctly on the real device.

- **The magnifying-glass button's real destination, finally — "Spotlight for P Corp OS."** It had been a documented no-op "reserved for future search" on both platforms since first built, never specced further beyond that phrase anywhere in the repo. Asked Joshua directly what it should search, since a separate, already-working conversation-content search exists via the clock-icon button (`GET /conversations?q=`) — confirmed he wants genuine cross-domain search, not a duplicate of that.

  New `backend/app/search.py`'s `search_all(query)` reuses each domain's existing `dashboard_snapshot()`/`list_*()` function rather than writing new queries — Knowledge (`list_docs()`), Alpha Mode Media (`alpha_mode_supabase.dashboard_snapshot()` — real Supabase projects/invoices/leads), Joshx (clients/leads/projects), Finance (accounts + holdings), Personal (goals/habits), Automations (rule name/description), and Conversations (folded in via the existing `list_conversations(query=...)`, one more result group in the same response). Each domain capped at 5 results, matched case-insensitively against real name/title fields, each domain's search wrapped independently so one failure (a Supabase hiccup) can't blank the rest. Deliberately excluded, not overlooked: Memory (no nav section to land on — it only feeds Frank's system prompt), Operations tasks (no "Operations" NavItem either), Triggers (5 fixed rule types, toggle-only), Trading Division (backtest run IDs, not named things), Calendar (a completely different data source, EventKit/AppleScript). New `GET /search?q=...` matches the existing `/conversations?q=` house style exactly.

  New shared `SearchResult` model and `SearchClient.swift` (`PCorpKit`) — a 300ms-debounced fetch, centralizing what `ConversationHistorySheet`/`ConversationListPopover` each already implement their own copy of, rather than adding a third. New `UniversalSearchSheet.swift` (iOS, same `.searchable` + grouped `List` pattern as `ConversationHistorySheet`) and `UniversalSearchPopover` (desktop, same manual-TextField + `LazyVStack` pattern as `ConversationListPopover`), both grouped by domain in the backend's own result order, not alphabetized. Tapping a result either calls `switchToConversation` (Conversations) or jumps `selectedID` to the matching nav section — desktop's `WarRoomView` never previously received `selectedID` at all; it now takes it as a `@Binding` the same way `RightRail` already does, rather than inventing a second convention. No keyboard shortcut bound to either platform's trigger — Cmd+K is already "Ask Frank" and Cmd+F is deliberately avoided (system Find conflict), both confirmed constraints from this exact feature's own history.

  Verified end to end against the real backend before touching either UI: `q=luno` correctly returned the real Luno Finance account with its actual holdings; `q=roadmap` returned the real Knowledge doc; a real Joshx client name ("Herbish") correctly returned both a `Joshx` group (client + project) and a `Conversations` group referencing two real past chats, in one response; empty/garbage queries correctly returned `[]`; a missing token correctly 422'd (existing house behavior — required-but-absent query params fail FastAPI's own validation before `verify_token` ever runs) and a wrong-but-present token correctly 403'd. Both apps rebuilt properly — desktop via `build_app.sh`, not plain `swift build` — and redeployed for Joshua to confirm live in the actual UI.

- **Design Agent and Operations Agent get the same real, approval-gated codebase access Engineering Agent proved out.** The explicit follow-up flagged when Engineering Agent shipped: "Design/Operations/Communications Agents are deliberately not wired into this mechanism yet." Checked all three named agents before scoping rather than assuming: Design (`design_agent.py`) and Operations (`operations_agent.py`) both have a real codebase to touch — SwiftUI source for Design, `automations_registry.py`/Knowledge docs for Operations. Communications Agent doesn't — its entire domain is external email/message drafts, no source of its own to read or edit — confirmed directly with Joshua to skip it this round rather than force-fitting something artificial onto it.

  Rather than duplicating Engineering Agent's inner tool-use loop a second and third time, extracted the reusable parts into new `backend/app/agent_codebase_tools.py` (rule of three — the first copy lived inline since it was the only consumer, a third confirms it's worth sharing): the 7 tool schemas, the dispatch logic, and a new shared `run_agentic_loop(system_prompt, initial_request, client, websocket, image=None)` generalizing Engineering Agent's own `while True: stream → dispatch tool_use → loop` shape to take any system prompt, plus an optional first-turn image for Design Agent's own multimodal need. `engineering_agent.py` shrinks to a thin wrapper with no behavior change; `design_agent.py`'s executor now calls the shared loop instead of a bespoke single-shot stream (its own `image` forwarding stays exactly as it was, just flows into the shared loop's first turn); `operations_agent.py`'s `consult_operations_agent` branch does the same, incidentally picking up the same `max_tokens`/adaptive-thinking fix Engineering Agent needed (its old flat `max_tokens=4096` had already once truncated a real SOP mid-document, per that file's own history).

  **A real gap caught immediately during verification, not shipped blind:** the first live test asked Frank to have the Design Agent read a real file — Frank refused, saying "the Design Agent doesn't have file/codebase access." Root cause: only each agent's *inner* system prompt had been updated to describe the new capability — the *outer* `consult_design_agent`/`consult_operations_agent` tool descriptions (the only thing Frank itself actually reads to decide when to delegate) still described the old, advice-only scope. Fixed by updating both outer tool descriptions to explicitly mention the real read/propose-edit access, matching how `consult_engineering_agent`'s own description already does. Re-verified immediately after: Design Agent correctly read `PCorpKit/Sources/PCorpKit/Style.swift` and reported `PCorpFont.label()`'s real defaults (10.5pt, semibold, rounded) — cross-checked exactly correct against the file; Operations Agent correctly read `automations_registry.py` and reported the real single existing rule by name — also cross-checked exactly correct. A real propose/reject cycle against Design Agent (a throwaway comment-line edit to `Style.swift`) correctly rejected with the file left genuinely untouched (`git status` clean). Design Agent's pre-existing image-critique feature re-verified unchanged after the refactor — sent a synthetic solid-red test image, got back `#DB1414` against a real `#DC1414`, confirming it's still genuinely looking at attached pixels, not guessing.

- **Joshx audit: surfaced already-stored data that never reached the dashboard, and found a real live duplicate-row bug.** Asked directly what was still missing from Joshx (Phase 1, 2026-08-21) — the honest answer split into deliberately-deferred future scope (invoicing, availability, crew/equipment — unchanged) versus a real, narrow bug: `dashboard_snapshot()`'s own SELECT statements under-selected columns that `add_joshx_client`/`add_joshx_lead`/`add_joshx_project` could already write — a client's email/phone/Instagram, a lead's budget, a project's brief, all genuinely stored, never once shown on either platform. Fixed by expanding the three SELECTs to every writable column, updating `JoshxClientRecord`/`JoshxLead`/`JoshxProject` (`PCorpKit/Models.swift`) to carry them, and adding a compact second/third line to each row on both platforms' `JoshxView.swift` showing exactly what's now returned.

  **While verifying live, Joshua caught a real duplicate: two identical "Malondie SS26" project rows.** Traced to `add_project` being a bare `INSERT` — calling it twice for the same project (re-confirming a booking, a slightly different phrasing) silently created a second row instead of updating the first, and the two rows had actually diverged (one carried a real budget + location notes, the other a shoot date) — a real, if minor, data-loss risk baked into the bug. Found genuinely uncommitted work already sitting in the file mid-fix from earlier the same day (a correct upsert implementation, but with a docstring dated one day in the future — "fixed 2026-08-28" against an actual date of 2026-08-27, and never committed) — finished it rather than redoing it: fixed the date, merged the two live duplicate rows by hand (keeping every real field from both), and applied the identical upsert pattern to `add_lead`, which had the same exposure (`leads` has no unique constraint at all, unlike `clients.name`, which is at least `UNIQUE` and would raise rather than silently duplicate). Verified via a direct `build_joshx_block()` call (no API cost) showing exactly 3 clean projects, no duplicates.

- **People/Relationships — a new domain, deliberately separate from Joshx and Alpha Mode Media clients.** First item of a six-part capability brief (Email/Calendar OAuth, mobile parity, auto-scheduling, and ambient capture are the other five, all deferred — two are blocked on Joshua creating real Google/Microsoft OAuth app credentials himself, which can't be done on his behalf). New `backend/app/people_db.py`/`people_tools.py` follow `joshx_db.py`'s exact shape: `add_person` (built as a real upsert from the very first version, directly applying what the Malondie bug above had just proven — no reason to reintroduce a just-fixed bug class in a brand-new domain), `log_interaction` (logs a contact event and bumps `last_contact_date` in one call, not two), `update_follow_up_cadence`, and `get_overdue_follow_ups` — the one genuinely nuanced query, treating a person as overdue if an explicit `next_follow_up_date` has passed, or (no explicit date) their own `follow_up_cadence_days` has lapsed since last contact, including someone with a cadence set who's never been contacted at all.

  Wired into the real Triggers Layer as a new `relationship_follow_up_overdue` rule — three required registration points (`triggers_db.py`'s `RULE_TYPES`, `triggers.py`'s `RULE_CHECKERS` and `SECTION_TITLES`), the last of which isn't optional: the daily digest's own formatter indexes `SECTION_TITLES` directly and would crash the first time this rule ever had a due item. Also wired into `insights.py`'s real "Frank's Insights" card, mirroring `_clients_needing_outreach()`'s exact shape and category. Shared `PeopleClient.swift` (PCorpKit) plus a new "PEOPLE" section added into the *existing* `PersonalView.swift` on both platforms, not a new nav destination — `Personal`'s own subtitle has read "Life & Relationships" since it was first built, while its content was only ever goals/habits; this closes that gap rather than opening a fourth "personal" screen.

- **Real Google OAuth, then Gmail + Google Calendar integration — items 2 and 3 of the same six-part brief, unblocked once Joshua created a real Google Cloud OAuth app and pasted its Client ID/Secret directly into chat** (stored in `backend/.env`, documented in `.env.example`). New shared `backend/app/google_oauth.py` — plain `httpx` against Google's OAuth endpoints directly, no `google-auth`/`google-api-python-client` (confirmed neither is a dependency; matches this codebase's own "plain REST, no heavy SDK" convention already used for Supabase). Requests both `gmail.readonly` and `calendar.readonly` in one grant; the refresh/access token pair lives in its own dedicated `backend/data/google_oauth_token.json`, never a row in a queryable SQLite table and never imported by any `*_tools.py` file, so no Frank tool can ever return it as a result. Three new unauthenticated routes (`/auth/google/start`, `/auth/google/callback`, `/auth/google/status`), same "unauthenticated at the route level, safe because the backend itself is loopback/Tailscale-only" reasoning already established for `/mobile` — the real security boundary here is Google's own login screen, not this app's local token.

  **Calendar is deliberately read-only this pass** — `google_calendar_client.py`/`calendar_db.py` only sync, they never write. The brief's own item 5 (auto-scheduling, still deferred) is where real calendar writes get a proper approval-gated mechanism later; adding ungated Google Calendar writes now would either pre-empt that design or create a second, inconsistent write path. `create_calendar_event`/`update_calendar_event`/`delete_calendar_event` (`calendar_tools.py`) stay exactly as they were, AppleScript-only. The one real change: `list_calendar_events` now reads from `calendar_db.py`'s merged cache instead of calling `system_calendar.list_events()` directly, so Frank's existing "what's on my calendar" question now sees both sources with zero new tool surfaced — it just knows more. AppleScript rows get a synthetic id (hash of title+start, since `system_calendar.py` confirmed there's no real event UID available through AppleScript at all — a disclosed limitation, not a bug); Google rows use Google's own real, stable event id. A new periodic `_calendar_sync_loop()` (`main.py`, 15-minute tick, same `hasattr(app.state, ...)` double-start guard already protecting `trigger_scheduler_task` under the dual-uvicorn-listener setup) keeps the cache warm so `list_calendar_events` reads instantly rather than paying Google API latency on every turn.

  **Email is read/log only, per the brief's own hard constraint — no send/compose path exists anywhere in `gmail_client.py`/`email_db.py`/`email_tools.py`, and the OAuth grant itself only ever requests the `.readonly` scope**, so there's no path by which this code could send anything even if some future change tried. `email_db.py` matches each synced message's sender against the only two real email-bearing fields anywhere in this app — `people.email` and Joshx `clients.email` (confirmed via grep: Alpha Mode Media has no email column anywhere, real Supabase schema included, so it isn't checked) — and bumps that person/client's `last_contact_date` on a match, same plain-text cross-database reference convention already established for `people.linked_client_name`. Deliberately no `build_email_block()` — unlike Joshx/Personal/People's small stable lists, email volume would bloat Frank's system prompt every single turn, so it's surfaced only when Frank actually calls `get_recent_emails`/`search_emails`, matching Shadow Mode's own "never proactively volunteer unasked" discipline.

  Verified end to end for real: completed the actual OAuth consent flow in a real browser against Joshua's own Google account (he chose the account and clicked Allow himself — that's not an automatable step); `/auth/google/status` confirmed connected; direct calls to `sync_recent_emails()`/`sync_calendar_cache()` pulled real data (10 real Gmail messages; 8 merged calendar events, correctly showing "BOS Activation - Rosebank" from both the AppleScript and Google sources at once, plus a Google-only "Learning reminder" with no AppleScript counterpart); then a full live conversation through Frank (real API credits) correctly summarized recent emails, correctly merged both calendar sources and proactively flagged a real scheduling conflict (Malondie videography booked all day Aug 29th, overlapping the multi-day BOS Activation window), and correctly refused a direct request to send an email, stating plainly it has no send capability at all.

  **Real regression found live minutes after shipping: Joshua reported Calendar.app "randomly reopening."** Root cause: `system_calendar.py`'s `_run_osascript` runs `open -a Calendar` before every single AppleScript call to guarantee the app is actually running (its own docstring already documents why — AppleScript's `launch` command inside a script isn't reliable enough alone). That was invisible when this path only fired on a user-initiated "what's on my calendar" question, but the new `_calendar_sync_loop()` (`main.py`, every 15 minutes, forever) now calls into this exact path in the background — and plain `open -a Calendar` activates/foregrounds an app even when it's already running, so Calendar.app was being yanked to the front every 15 minutes with zero user interaction. Fixed with one flag: `open -g -a Calendar` still launches the app if it isn't running (AppleScript automation still needs it open), but never steals focus doing so. Verified live: triggered the exact sync path manually and confirmed the frontmost app never changed. Backend restarted via its real launchd service (`media.alphamode.pcorpos.backend`) to pick up the fix — a second, unrelated thing caught mid-restart: an earlier `pkill -f "app.main"` used while testing this same feature had raced the supervised process for port 8731 and left it wedged in a half-crashed state (a real `[Errno 48] address already in use` on its own Tailscale bind, from `main.py`'s own `_run_dual`); cleared by killing the wedged PID and letting launchd relaunch cleanly, confirmed healthy afterward.

  Built and verified with zero API credits spent — the account ran out again mid-session (confirmed via a direct SDK call returning the same real "credit balance too low" error as before, not a bug: earlier websocket tests that looked like silent hangs were actually the SDK retrying quietly rather than failing fast). Every piece of this was verified directly instead: `add_person` called twice confirmed one merged row, not two; `get_overdue_follow_ups` tested against five synthetic people covering all three overdue conditions plus two that correctly weren't flagged; `GET /people/dashboard` and `GET /triggers/status` both curl-tested showing the new rule correctly registered, correctly labeled, and correctly flagging real due items with no crash. Test data cleaned up before shipping. Full live-through-Frank verification is still pending credits, but the UI path itself is confirmed real end to end: added Joshua's actual first real entry directly via `add_person` (Robin, spouse, 14-day cadence, bypassing the LLM since credits are still out) and he confirmed it rendering correctly in the real iOS app on his own device.

## 2026-08-30

- **iOS app started showing "no longer available" — a free Apple ID's 7-day provisioning profile expiry, not a bug.** The embedded profile (checked directly: `security cms -D -i embedded.mobileprovision`) had been created 2026-08-20 and expired 2026-08-27, exactly the free-tier limit Xcode enforces without a paid Apple Developer Program membership. Fixed by rebuilding with `xcodebuild -allowProvisioningUpdates` (auto-renews the profile) and reinstalling via `xcrun devicectl device install app` + `launch`. One extra step needed since the *certificate* itself had also just been regenerated: iOS refused the first launch with a code-signature/entitlements error until Joshua manually trusted it once under Settings → General → VPN & Device Management — a one-time step tied to the certificate (valid a full year), not something that should recur on next week's routine profile-only renewal. Joshua's call: don't enroll in the paid program, just rebuild on demand each time this happens — recorded as its own memory since it's expected, recurring maintenance, not something worth re-diagnosing from scratch every week.

- **Auto-scheduling — item 5 of the six-part brief: calendar writes are now approval-gated, the same way Engineering/Design/Operations Agents' file edits already are.** `create_calendar_event`/`update_calendar_event`/`delete_calendar_event` used to write to the real macOS Calendar the instant Frank called them, no confirmation at all — `calendar_tools.py`'s own docstring had already named this exact gap ("writes stay AppleScript-only until item 5's approval-gated auto-scheduling exists"). Renamed to `propose_create_calendar_event`/`propose_update_calendar_event`/`propose_delete_calendar_event` specifically so the tool name itself signals the new behavior to Frank, matching `propose_file_edit`'s own naming convention. Each now sends a real approval card over the websocket and blocks on a decision before touching anything — copied directly from `agent_codebase_tools.py`'s `_propose_file_edit` mechanics (same sentinel/blocking-receive/id-matching pattern) rather than factored into a shared helper, since this is only the second approval flow ever built and a shared abstraction can wait for a third occurrence, same "rule of three" already applied when that module itself was extracted. `list_calendar_events` is untouched, still read-only. No before/after diff like file edits get — there's no existing "look up this event's current fields" helper, so the card shows the proposed change's own fields rather than a computed diff; a disclosed simplification, not an oversight.

  Swift side: `PCorpKit/Sources/PCorpKit/BackendClient.swift` gained a parallel `CalendarApprovalRequest`/`pendingCalendarApproval`/`respondToCalendarApproval` lane alongside the existing file-edit one — a separate struct and published property rather than generalizing the two into one shape, same "second occurrence, not a third" reasoning as the backend side, though the wire *response* format is reused as-is (`{"approval_response": {"id":, "approved":}}`) since the backend only ever matches by `id` regardless of which kind of approval it's blocking on. New `CalendarApprovalCard` view, hand-ported near-identically on both `desktop/Sources/PCorpOS/WarRoomView.swift` and `ios/P Corp OS/WarRoomView.swift` (same convention as the existing file-edit card, which isn't shared either) — same neutral, non-alert styling as its file-edit sibling ("a decision request, not a risk alert"), shown in the same spot above the input bar, which now disables on either approval kind being pending. Confirmed the "Calendar" nav tab itself is a pure local read-only calendar reader with zero connection to the backend at all on either platform — deliberately not touched; the proposal surfaces in the War Room chat, the same place Frank already proposes things conversationally.

  Verified end to end: direct websocket tests (bypassing the LLM) confirmed the exact approval-request payload shape for all three tools, confirmed a reject correctly left the calendar untouched, confirmed an approve correctly created/moved/cancelled a real test event, then cleaned up. One real mistake caught and corrected during live UI verification: attempted to drive the desktop app's own text input via blind AppleScript coordinate-clicking to screenshot the approval card myself — the click missed the target window, and the simulated keystrokes landed in Claude Code's own chat input instead, submitting the test prompt as a phantom message rather than reaching PCorpOS at all. No app state was touched by the mistake; caught immediately, explained plainly, and verification handed back to a safe path — Joshua tested it himself live on the real desktop app (card rendered correctly, Approve created a real event on the real calendar) rather than trusting further blind automation. Test event cleaned up afterward.

- **Real bug found live, same evening: Joshua's iPhone stopped getting replies from Frank at all, twice in a row, with a `[connection error: The request timed out]` message.** Backend was confirmed fully healthy throughout (health check 200 on both localhost and the Tailscale address; a direct minimal Claude API call returned instantly — not a repeat of the earlier credit-exhaustion issue). Root cause was client-side, in `PCorpKit/Sources/PCorpKit/BackendClient.swift`'s `send()`: its `task.send(...)` completion handler only ever *displayed* an error on failure — it never tore down `task` or set `isConnected = false`.
